A later commit (`c154c3e`) adopted the manifest design and deleted the env-var handoff shipped in `de22ce4`; that adoption was not the chosen outcome and is undone here by a forward revert (no history was rewritten — XACA-0798's commits sit beneath the tip and had already been fetched by other sessions). **Why the env var wins on the property that actually matters — staleness surface.** The handoff is scoped to the `restart` dispatcher and lives only in the environment of the `start` process that `restart` `exec`s; it dies with that process. There is no artifact left behind, so there is no window in which a *later*, unrelated command can read it. The manifest inverted that: it persisted the pre-teardown topology to `~/.aiteamforge/run/lcars-restart-manifest` bounded only by a 600-second TTL, and `start_lcars()` unioned that file in unconditionally — so a standalone `aiteamforge start` issued within ten minutes of *any* stop would resurrect teams the operator had **deliberately** stopped. The manifest's headline advantages were bounded by the same tradeoff: surviving a reboot and covering a manual `stop`-then-`start` are only desirable if restoring the old topology is always what the operator wanted, and for a deliberate `stop` it is precisely what they did not want. A restart outage is self-healing within 300s via `lcars-health`; a `start` that silently reverses an intentional `stop` is not, and gives the operator no signal that it happened. **Removed:** `libexec/lib/lcars-restart-manifest.sh` (427 lines) and the capture hook in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-stop.sh` — `stop` is once again side-effect-free and writes no runtime state. **Restored byte-identically to `de22ce4`:** `aiteamforge_team_for_lcars_port()` (`libexec/lib/aiteamforge-paths.sh`), `aiteamforge_lcars_running_ports()` (`libexec/lib/common.sh`), the snapshot-and-export in `bin/aiteamforge-cli.sh`'s `restart` branch, the union plus post-union early return in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-start.sh`, and the 12-assertion `tests/test-xaca-0799-restart-symmetry.sh`. Note on the three defects `c154c3e` claimed to fold in from its review: all three were defects in the **manifest** implementation, not in `de22ce4` — the restored code already reads PIDs via `while IFS= read -r` (never `for pid in $pids`, so no zsh word-splitting trap), already anchors port extraction on `server\.py` rather than scanning right-to-left for the last numeric token (so `server.py 8203 --workers 2` yields `8203`, not `2`), and iterates every array through the `${arr[@]+"${arr[@]}"}` form that is both split-safe and empty-safe under `set -u`. Nothing was lost in the revert. **`tests/ci-manifest` keeps its `test-xaca-0799-restart-symmetry.sh  plain-shell` line** — that entry was a genuine gap-fix in `c154c3e` (the test file `de22ce4` added was never classified), and the test file still exists, so dropping the line would fail `tests/ci-manifest-check.sh`'s completeness gate. **Verified after revert:** 12/12 on the XACA-0799 suite (matching `de22ce4`'s documented count) and 147/147 across `test-lifecycle`, `test-cli`, `test-xaca-0792-team-instance-id`, `test-config`, `test-teams`, `test-multi-team`; `bash -n` and `zsh -n` clean on all five changed shell files; `ci-manifest-check.sh` back to exactly its pre-existing two unrelated gaps. **Consumer impact: none** — develop's gitlink never advanced past `01700a9`, so neither implementation ever shipped.
- XACA-0799 — **`aiteamforge restart lcars` took the whole box's LCARS down to bring a subset back up.** The two halves of a restart had mismatched scope. `stop_lcars()` matches `server\.py [0-9]` with **no port** — deliberately, so `stop` is a kill-all (the matcher carries an explicit XACA-0560-001 warning against narrowing it) — and therefore reaps **every** LCARS server on the machine. `start_lcars()` iterates only `get_configured_teams()`, i.e. `.aiteamforge-config`'s `.teams[]`. On any box where most LCARS servers are launched by their own per-team `*-startup.sh`, those teams are **absent from `.teams[]`**, so a restart killed all of them and started only the configured handful; the rest stayed down until the 300s `com.aiteamforge.lcars-health` check healed them — a fleet-wide LCARS outage of up to five minutes on **every** restart. Observed on M4Mini immediately after the v0.17.7 upgrade (2026-07-13), i.e. *with* the XACA-0792 fix already in place: the upgrade refreshed `lcars-ui` → `lcars-watch` fired `aiteamforge restart lcars` → 8 servers were killed, only `finance-personal` (8361) came back, and ios/android/firebase/academy/dns/command/legal were all DOWN until the health check restored them. XACA-0792 fixed the *other* half of this asymmetry (start skipped project-scoped teams because it handed a base id to a registry keyed by instance id); this is the remaining half — the blast radius of stop still did not match the coverage of start. **Fix (snapshot & restore):** the `restart` branch of `bin/aiteamforge-cli.sh` now snapshots the ports that are actually **serving**, *before* the teardown, and exports them as `AITEAMFORGE_RESTORE_LCARS_PORTS`; `start_lcars()` maps each port back to its team through a new registry reverse lookup and unions those teams into its launch list. Ordering is the whole fix — snapshotting after `stop` returns an empty set and restores nothing while looking correct in a diff. **Deliberately scoped to `restart` only:** `stop` keeps its documented kill-all semantics and a standalone `start` keeps its configured-teams-only semantics, so neither standalone command changes behaviour; with the variable unset, `start` is byte-for-byte the old code path. The alternative fix — narrowing `stop` to the configured set — was rejected because it trades a restart outage for a silently broken `stop`-all, exactly what XACA-0560-001 warns against. **New helpers:** `aiteamforge_team_for_lcars_port()` in `libexec/lib/aiteamforge-paths.sh` (implemented as a scan over the *forward* lookup rather than an independent `jq` query, so the reverse can never claim a team/port pairing the forward lookup would not assign — a separate query could drift on config-vs-baked-in-defaults precedence or null-port handling and resurrect a server on the wrong port), and `aiteamforge_lcars_running_ports()` in `libexec/lib/common.sh`, whose `pgrep` pattern is contractually pinned to `stop_lcars()`'s matcher since its entire meaning is "the set stop is about to kill". The empty-`.teams[]` early return in `start_lcars()` was moved to sit **after** the union: ahead of it, a box with no configured teams bails out before restoring and the outage returns in full with the restore code present but unreachable. **Verified** by `tests/test-xaca-0799-restart-symmetry.sh` (12 assertions): reverse-lookup round-trip against the forward lookup for every registry team; an unowned port returning nonzero *and* empty (a bogus id would be worse than skipping); a real spawned `server.py <port>` process detected by the scan and dropped once it exits; the M4Mini shape pinned, showing the running set resolves teams the configured set does not contain; and structural guards on snapshot-before-stop ordering, the post-union placement of the early return, and stop's matcher staying port-less. **Two non-vacuity controls, because this repo has a documented vacuous-green trap:** an in-file negative control asserting the structural checks fail against a synthesized pre-fix copy, and the suite run against the genuinely reverted tree — 8 of 12 assertions fail there, exit 1. Development also surfaced a portability bug worth pinning: the first cut used `for pid in $pids`, and because **zsh does not word-split unquoted parameter expansions**, the loop ran once over the whole PID blob, every `ps` lookup failed, and the function returned an empty port set while still exiting 0 — a vacuous success that would have handed `restart` nothing to restore and silently reinstated the entire bug. It now reads PIDs via `while IFS= read -r`, and Case 10 fails any regression to the splitting form (asserted against comment-stripped source, since the fix documents the broken form by name). **PR #712 review round (4 findings ported onto this design; 3 resolved or moot).** The review ran a 7-mutation pass and found the suite's non-vacuity claim covered only helpers — `start_lcars()`'s restore dedupe was reachable solely by launching real servers, so DELETING it outright kept the suite green. It is now `aiteamforge_restore_key_is_new()` in `lib/common.sh`, driven directly; the same is true of the port extraction, previously a sed re-typed into the test body (proving only that the test's own copy worked) and now `aiteamforge_extract_lcars_port()`. The reverse lookup cost — measured 1.35s added to every restart at 15 teams x 8 ports, sitting in front of a teardown `lcars-watch` fires on every `lcars-ui` change — came from scanning every team and forking jq twice per team, per port; `aiteamforge_lcars_port_team_map()` now builds the whole map in ONE jq pass, `start_lcars()` builds it once outside the loop, and lookups are fork-free. **A first attempt memoized inside the map builder and was deleted as dead code:** every caller invokes it through `$( ... )`, which runs in a SUBSHELL, so the cache never survived back to the parent — it looked like an optimisation and was not, and the test written for it could not tell the difference. That is why the placement is now pinned structurally (Case 14e) rather than behaviorally. The map is also READ-ONLY, closing a smaller hazard the per-team path carried: it went through `aiteamforge_team_lcars_port()`, which MATERIALIZES a default registry on first lookup, so a reverse lookup could create state just by running (the XACA-0792-001 precedent from `status`). Matcher-equivalence now pins every kill/capture site (stop, uninstall, migrate, capture) to one spelling instead of 2 of 5; `aiteamforge-doctor.sh` is explicitly excluded and named, since it probes with a deliberately broader port-less pattern. Suite 12 → **25**, with all three fixes mutation-verified: deleting the dedupe kills 3 cases, reverting the extractor kills 2, and moving the map build inside the loop kills 1 — each restoring to 25/25. One caveat recorded honestly: the first run of that last control appeared to SURVIVE, and the mutation had simply failed to apply; it was re-run with the edit verified present before being believed. **PR #714 review round — two guards were defeatable and two real defects shipped.** Mutation testing by the review gate showed two of this suite's own guards reporting green while broken: Case 6's ordering anchor used a file-GLOBAL `grep -n aiteamforge-stop.sh | tail -1`, so moving the snapshot AFTER the teardown and appending a trailing comment naming that path pushed the anchor past it and the suite stayed at 25/25 with the exact regression present; and Case 15's matcher-equivalence check had no per-file floor, so widening `common.sh`'s capture matcher to a port-less `pgrep -f "server\.py"` — the precise violation its own CONTRACT comment forbids — simply removed that file from the result set and left the set size at 1. Case 6 now anchors on the first `aiteamforge-stop.sh` invocation at or after the `restart)` label; Case 15 requires each of the four named files to contribute at least one match. Case 7 was likewise comment-satisfiable (both its needles appear in start.sh's own prose) and now strips comments like its siblings. **Two genuine defects, both introduced by the previous round's own fixes.** (1) The single-pass jq map covered a strictly NARROWER set than the forward lookup it must round-trip with: jq reads team-paths.json only, while `aiteamforge_team_lcars_port` falls back to the baked-in defaults, so a team absent from the config resolved FORWARD yet was invisible to the REVERSE map — its live port hit "no team owns it" and the team was silently dropped from a restart. The map now fills those gaps through the forward lookup, and only for teams jq missed, so the common case still costs one fork. (2) `AITEAMFORGE_RESTORE_LCARS_PORTS` was exported and never unset, so it leaked down the whole process tree — and start.sh's own preflight invokes `aiteamforge-doctor.sh`, whose remediation runs `aiteamforge start kanban`. That nested STANDALONE start would inherit the snapshot and restore teams outside any restart: precisely the "a standalone start reverses a deliberate stop" failure mode for which the manifest design was rejected, reintroduced through the back door. It is now unset immediately after consumption. Also added the missing fail-soft degrade coverage the design's central safety claim rested on but nothing asserted, and corrected docstrings still describing a deleted mtime cache and a forward-scan implementation that is now an independent jq query. Suite 25 → **29**; all five fixes mutation-verified with the edit's presence confirmed before each run, after an earlier control was found to have silently failed to apply. **PR #714 review round 3 — the previous round pinned the wrong half, and two stated claims were wrong.** The gate's mutation pass found FOUR regressions still passing with the suite green, all in `start_lcars()`'s restore loop: deleting the dedupe **call** (round 2 extracted the predicate into a testable function and left its call site unpinned — proving the function worked while the loop could stop using it), `teams+=` becoming `teams=` so a restore REPLACED the configured list instead of adding to it, the unowned-port warn+continue replaced by a synthesized team id (which would be launched with a bogus `LCARS_TEAM`), and the safe `read -ra` split swapped for an unquoted `($VAR)`. None was reachable without launching real servers. The entire decision now lives in `aiteamforge_build_restore_union()` — configured ids in, final launch list out, warnings on stderr — so all four are directly assertable; each is now mutation-verified to kill (2, 2, 1 and 1 cases respectively). Suite 29 → **37**. **Two corrections to this entry's own earlier text.** (1) The claim "deleting the dedupe kills 3 cases" described a different mutation than the one originally reported and measured 2, not 3. (2) The stated threat model for unsetting `AITEAMFORGE_RESTORE_LCARS_PORTS` was wrong on two counts: `run_first_launch_preflight` runs near the top of `aiteamforge-start.sh`, BEFORE dispatch reaches `start_lcars`, so the unset is too late to protect that path — and `aiteamforge-doctor.sh` already refuses to auto-start while in `--preflight` mode, so the cited chain cannot fire either way. The unset is still correct and retained: it protects the per-team `*-startup.sh` launches that follow the union, and anything they invoke. Only the justification was wrong. Also extended the XACA-0799-010 config-absent fill to the **no-jq fallback branch**, which still carried the narrower coverage (bounded in practice by the formula's `depends_on "jq"`, but it was the same defect in the sibling path), and swept the comment-defeatable `grep -n <needle> | head -1` anchors out of Case 8 and Case 14e — the class Case 6 was fixed for one instance of, rather than as a class.
- XACA-0838 — **`kb-restart`'s hardcoded `TEAM_PORTS` array in the shipped `kanban-helpers.template.sh` had rotted well past its two originally-reported stale freelance entries.** Measured against the live `team-paths.json` registry: `freelance-liquidstyle-agentbadges-app`/`-ios` were stale by a whole port-band renumber (`8960`/`8970` vs. actual `8508`/`8509`); `freelance-doublenode-starwords`/`-workstats`/`-appplanning` were already wrong before this ticket touched anything (`8505` hardcoded vs. actual `8504`/`8506`/`8500`); and `freelance-bandwear-android` — a genuinely registered team — was absent from the array entirely, so `kb-restart` on it failed "Unknown team" even though the team exists and has a valid port. A hardcoded per-client table can never track `kb-init-team` provisioning new freelance instances at runtime — this was the last carrier of that pattern; `dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh` solved the same drift class for the same function already (XACA-0628's overlay-first freelance lookup). **Fix:** ripped the array out. `kb-restart()` now calls a new `_kb_team_lcars_port()`, a minimal in-template mirror of `dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh`'s function of the same name (that file is dev-tree-only and never ships to consumers, so the resolution strategy has to live here too, not a divergent reimplementation): freelance-* slugs resolve via `_kb_overlay_lookup` against the `team-paths.json` overlay (gated on the entry carrying its own `team_code`, the marker that distinguishes a self-describing registration from a legacy stub); every other team prefers the canonical `aiteamforge_team_lcars_port` loader (sourced at the top of this file from `libexec/lib/aiteamforge-paths.sh`); a small built-in case table is the final fallback for the well-known non-freelance teams (`ios`/`android`/`firebase`/`academy`/`dns`/`freelance`/`command`/`mainevent`) this file has always shipped, only reached when the loader itself is unavailable. The unknown-team path now returns a clean error (`Error: Unknown team '$team' (no LCARS port configured)`) instead of letting an empty port reach `curl`/`pkill`. Verified by extracting the two new functions and sourcing them standalone against the real `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json`: all three renumbered/newly-covered freelance teams resolve correctly (`freelance-bandwear-android` → `8505`, `freelance-liquidstyle-agentbadges-app` → `8508`, `-ios` → `8509`), the three previously-wrong doublenode slugs now resolve to their *actual* current ports with zero code changes needed for them specifically (the overlay lookup is self-correcting), every non-freelance team preserves its exact prior hardcoded value when the canonical loader is unavailable, and an unregistered team returns empty output with a non-zero exit rather than a bad port. `xaca-0139:allowed` debrand markers preserved on the surviving `mainevent` case arm; the `xaca-0139-debrand-guard.bats` violation count went DOWN (503 → 501 lines) after this change, confirming no new unmarked brand references were introduced. **Known follow-up, explicitly out of scope here:** the built-in fallback table still returns `mainevent`/`command` → `8234`, matching this template's pre-existing (already-stale) value — `dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh`'s own `_kb_team_lcars_port()` fixed this to `8400` under XACA-0727/0463, but that fix never reached this template. In normal operation (canonical loader present, which it always is on any real consumer install) this template now correctly resolves `mainevent` to `8400` via the loader tier — the stale `8234` only surfaces in the degenerate case where the loader itself is missing, a state in which most other kb-* functionality is already broken. Not fixed here to keep this change scoped to the freelance port-drift class it was filed for.
- XACA-0814 — **`aiteamforge upgrade` never regenerated per-team `*-connect.sh` / `*-disconnect.sh` cockpit scripts, so a fix to the connect-script generator itself never reached a machine that had already installed under the broken generator.** `install-team.sh --connect-only` renders `${INSTANCE_ID}-connect.sh` / `${INSTANCE_ID}-disconnect.sh`, but its only call sites are `bin/aiteamforge-setup.sh`'s cockpit-mode pass and its full-mode "render remaining teams" pass — both reached only from `aiteamforge setup`, never from `aiteamforge upgrade`. XACA-0785 fixed a defect in that generator, but the upgrade run-sequence had no step that ever re-ran it, so every consumer box that installed before XACA-0785 shipped kept its stale, pre-fix connect/disconnect scripts forever — `aiteamforge upgrade` refreshed every other component and silently skipped these. Same bug class as the XACA-0608 / XACA-0673 / XACA-0751 / XACA-0771 install-vs-upgrade gaps (5th sighting). **Fix:** new `update_connect_scripts()` in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh`, registered in the run sequence immediately after `update_team_scripts`. Refresh-only (a team whose connect script this machine never rendered is skipped, never materialized) — the guard globs `${team_id}*-connect.sh` against `WORKING_DIR` so it matches both a bare instance id and an XACA-0485 project-augmented one (e.g. `finance` → `finance-personal-connect.sh`). Delegates the actual render to `install-team.sh --connect-only` in an isolated `bash` subprocess, mirroring the setup wizard's own cockpit loop exactly — INSTANCE_ID computation is never duplicated here. DRY_RUN-aware and fail-soft (a single team's render failure warns and continues; never aborts the unattended nightly auto-upgrade). New regression suite `tests/test-xaca-0814-upgrade-connect-scripts.sh` (10/10): structural assertions that the function is defined and wired into the run sequence (the assertion that would have caught this bug), plus sandboxed functional coverage of the refresh-only guard, DRY_RUN, missing-teams-dir / missing-installer fail-soft paths, the project-augmented instance-id glob match, and installer-failure fail-softness.
- XACA-0831 (XACA-0818 follow-up) — **The atomic knowledge-ID allocator `_kb_alloc_slot` was never mirrored into either tap copy — and it was a PARTIAL mirror, which is worse than a clean miss.** XACA-0818 shipped two mechanisms to end the scan→compute→create TOCTOU race that lets concurrent `kb-knowledge-add` callers mint duplicate `<prefix>NNN` slots: **Mechanism B**, a fleet duplicate-slot *detector* in `share/scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh` (Guard 4 / `_dup_slots`), which HAD been mirrored (commit `74312d2`, the entry directly below); and **Mechanism A**, the `_kb_alloc_slot` allocator that *prevents* the collision by serializing the whole critical section under an exclusive per-dir `flock`, which had not. Consumers therefore ran the alarm without the remedy — `kb-knowledge-sync` would report duplicate slots it had no code path to stop producing. Measured pre-fix: canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` had 8 `_kb_alloc_slot` occurrences; both `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` and `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh` had **0**, still on the pre-0818 `new_file="${target_dir}/${entry_id}.md"` string assignment. **Ported into both copies:** the `_kb_alloc_slot` function **byte-identical** to canonical (its lock deliberately kept at `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/kb-alloc-${dir_hash}.lock` — OUTSIDE the synced knowledge tree, keyed by target-dir hash, so it can neither wedge `kb-knowledge-sync`'s dirty check nor leak into the fleet root; a faithful copy IS the consumer-safe version), plus **both** call sites — `kb-knowledge-add` (scan/compute block replaced with the atomic alloc + id-derivation-from-reserved-filename) and `kb-knowledge-promote`'s auto-allocation path (guarded by a new `target_entry_auto` flag so an explicitly named target keeps its caller-supplied id and only an auto-allocated one is re-resolved atomically). **Verified** by a new `tests/test-xaca-0831-alloc-slot-atomicity.sh` (15/15): 25 concurrent `_kb_alloc_slot` calls fired at one shared dir against BOTH copies assert distinct + contiguous 1..25 NNN slots and Mechanism B reporting zero duplicates, with a negative control (a hand-built `k002`/`k002` collision) empirically proven to make the detector bite. `zsh -n` clean on both files; the ported function diffs byte-identical against canonical in all three copies. A companion drift guard on the dev-team side (`tests/test-knowledge-helper-parity.sh`) now fails CI if this class recurs.
- XACA-0818 — **Fleet duplicate-ID-slot backstop in the knowledge sync path.** `share/scripts/kb-knowledge-sync.sh` gains a post-pull hard gate: after the rebase, a cross-host merge that produced two entries sharing one `kNNN` slot now fails loudly (exit 65) instead of silently pushing the collision. Mirrors the atomic per-directory allocation lock added to `kb-knowledge-add`/`-promote` (the within-host half of the same fix).
- XACA-0802 (PR #699 BLOCKER) — **The host-affinity guard shipped into a file consumers never install. Ported into the one they do.** The previous pass landed the guard in `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`, which reads like the consumer helper but is **dead fallback code**: both `libexec/installers/install-kanban.sh` and `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-upgrade.sh` PREFER `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh` and only fall through to the template when the aliases file is **absent**. It is git-tracked and always shipped, so the `if` branch always wins and the template is never installed on a fresh box. Net effect was verbatim the XACA-0779 failure this ticket exists to fix: `aiteamforge_paths.py` **is** mirrored, so a consumer backfilled `primary_host` into its overlay and the `kanban-helpers.sh` actually running there had nothing that reads it — and the machine holding the three PII teams (`finance-personal`, `legal-coparenting`, `medical-general`) was precisely the machine with no guard. Measured before the fix: the aliases file had **0** occurrences of `primary_host` / `host_affinity` / `allow-foreign-host` / `val_roots` / `Duplicate ID slot`, against **8** for `_kb_knowledge_local_root` — it routed PII to the never-syncing local root with nothing guarding the route, and its `kb-knowledge-validate` printed only `Global root:`, never walking the local root and never detecting a duplicate slot. **Ported into `kanban-aliases.sh`:** the six helpers (`_kb_host_normalize`, `_kb_this_host_id`, `_kb_team_primary_host`, `_kb_host_matches`, `_kb_team_for_local_persona`, `_kb_knowledge_host_affinity_guard`); both `kb-knowledge-add` call sites (agent + team tiers) placed after local-only routing and **before** `mkdir -p` / entry-id allocation so a refusal leaves no directory and no file; `--allow-foreign-host` parsing wired alongside the existing `--force`, with usage text; and the full two-root `kb-knowledge-validate` (`_kb_val_collect_root` applied to both roots, the parallel `val_roots` array tagging findings `[global]`/`[local]` **and** resolving each entry's cross-refs against its OWN root, silent skip of an absent local root, same-root de-duplication labelled `(same as global root — validated once)`, and the hard ERROR on a duplicate `<prefix>NNN` slot). The file's existing local-only region was diffed against canonical first so its deliberate divergences survive untouched — notably its `_kb_get_team`-based team resolution in place of `_kb_detect_context` (this file is context-safe by design and has no tmux/`.kb-team` chain) and its `KANBAN_TEAM` rather than `KB_TEAM` remediation hint. All six helpers' dependencies (`_kb_overlay_config_path`, `_kb_is_local_persona`, `_kb_is_local_only_team`, `_kb_local_only_teams_hardcoded`, `_kb_local_personas_for_team`) were already present, so no partial port was needed. **Verified by sourcing a placeholder-substituted copy of the shipped aliases file** (0 tokens remaining) in a fully sandboxed tree (`KB_KNOWLEDGE_GLOBAL_ROOT` / `KB_KNOWLEDGE_LOCAL_ROOT` / `AITEAMFORGE_CONFIG` / `KB_KNOWLEDGE_PROJECT_PATH` all redirected to `mktemp` dirs): a foreign-host local-only write refuses non-zero, names both the owning host and the override flag, and leaves **no directory and zero `.md` files** behind; the owning host proceeds with **0 bytes on stderr** and the entry lands in the local root with nothing leaking to global; a fleet-synced team with no declared host is **byte-silent**; an undeclared local-only team fails **open** with exactly one warning; and a seeded duplicate `t001` in the LOCAL root is a `[FAIL]`, exits non-zero, names both colliding files, and proves the local root was walked (`[local]` tag) — with a negative control removing one duplicate and returning validate to a clean exit 0.
- XACA-0802-005 (PR #699 review) — **Added a drift gate over all three copies of the region, because this defect shipped twice in a row.** `sync-tap.sh` has no `sync_file` mapping for `kanban-helpers.template.sh` **or** `kanban-aliases.sh`, so nothing has ever compared either against canonical — which is why the guard could land first in a file that was not mirrored at all, then in a file consumers do not install, with no gate objecting either time. New `scripts/tests/test-knowledge-region-parity.sh` (26 assertions) extracts and comment-strips the six host-affinity helpers and `kb-knowledge-validate` in full from canonical `kanban-helpers.sh`, `kanban-helpers.template.sh` and `kanban-aliases.sh`, and fails on any divergence with a message naming the diverged copy, the function, and the comment-stripped diff. Comments are deliberately excluded: the tap copies follow a standing editorial rule (code bodies identical, comments reframed to drop dev-box specifics while keeping XACA ids), and comparing them would fight that rule. `kb-knowledge-add` is compared on its XACA-0802 lines only — the rest legitimately differs (reworded `--force`/usage text, `KANBAN_TEAM` vs `KB_TEAM`), and comparing it whole would produce a permanent false failure and get the gate disabled. Three absolute-floor presence checks per copy pin the guard at exactly 2 call sites, the `--allow-foreign-host` usage line, and the duplicate-slot ERROR. **Two negative controls, because this repo has a documented vacuous-green trap:** a seeded code divergence in `_kb_host_normalize` must be detected (it is), and the gate was run against the actual pre-fix tree — it reports 11 failures with actionable text (`MISSING from share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh — the region was never ported into that copy`), confirming it would have caught the shipped defect. That second run also caught a bug in the gate itself: `assert_present` was invoked with a stray `--` that shifted the pattern, so the `--allow-foreign-host` check matched everything and passed on a tree that lacked the flag entirely; fixed with `grep -e`.
- XACA-0802-006 (PR #699 review) — **A parity test passed only because of call order.** `test_python_and_shell_agree_on_every_primary_host_shape` asserted the Python accessor and the shell guard agree on absent / empty / hand-set `primary_host`, but its "absent" case read Python **first**, and that read is not pure — `get_team_primary_host()` falls back to `DEFAULT_TEAMS` *and backfills the value to disk*. The shell then read the already-materialized file and matched. Reading shell-first reproduces `shell='' python='darren-m4-mini'`. The test asserted agreement it never established. **Fix:** the agreement test is now parametrized over **both** read orders, with the absent case explicitly primed so what it asserts is the steady state ("once materialized, the two consumers never diverge"); and the pre-backfill window — where the two legitimately disagree, because the shell has no `DEFAULT_TEAMS` and reads only the file — is asserted on its own in a new `test_absent_key_diverges_until_the_backfill_materializes_it`, which reads shell-first against untouched bytes, confirms `''`, then proves one Python read materializes the key on disk and the shell sees it permanently thereafter. That disagreement window IS the XACA-0779 exposure the backfill exists to close, so it is now stated rather than papered over by call order. Verified non-vacuous: removing the priming makes the `shell-first` parameter fail with the exact diagnosis while `python-first` still passes — demonstrating the old test's assurance was order-dependent.
- XACA-0802-004 (PR #699 review) — **`get_team_primary_host()` disagreed with the shell guard about an explicitly blanked `primary_host`.** `diff_missing_primary_host` / `apply_primary_host` treat **key presence** as the contract: a present key — including `"primary_host": ""` — means "the operator has spoken", and is never overwritten. The accessor did not honour that. It used a truthiness fallback (`if not host: host = DEFAULT_TEAMS[...]`), so an entry carrying an explicit empty string returned the DEFAULT_TEAMS host, while the shell guard reading the identical bytes (`jq '.teams[$t].primary_host // ""'`) read it as undeclared and failed **open**. Two consumers, one overlay, opposite verdicts. Worse, the fallback was **unreachable for the case it was written for**: `load_config()` always backfills before any read, so genuine absence never survives to the accessor — the fallback's only reachable effect was the wrong one, and an operator blanking the field to mean "no owner, let anyone author" got Python insisting a specific box owned it. **Fix:** the `DEFAULT_TEAMS` fallback is now key-presence-aware (`if "primary_host" in entry`), firing only on genuine absence. Dropping the fallback outright was the alternative; key-presence-awareness was chosen because it preserves the file's existing idiom — the same rule `diff_missing_primary_host` and `apply_primary_host` already encode — and keeps the migration tolerance `get_team_code` has for an overlay the backfill could not rewrite. Locked in by three new tests asserting `""` reads as undeclared through **both** paths (the Python accessor, and the real shell function sourced from `kanban-helpers.sh` against the same sandboxed file), across absent / empty / hand-set shapes, plus a negative control modelling the removed truthiness shape. The pair-equality assertion is the point: it fails if either side drifts.
- XACA-0802 — **`kb-knowledge-validate` header no longer reports a present root as "absent".** When `KB_KNOWLEDGE_LOCAL_ROOT` resolves to the same path as the global root, the collector de-duplicates (correctly, so entries are not validated and counted twice) but the header printed `(absent — skipped)` for a root that was neither absent nor skipped. It now says `(same as global root — validated once)`. Cosmetic, but a status line that misdescribes what happened is how someone concludes their local knowledge went unvalidated. Fixed in the canonical helpers and the consumer template together.
- XACA-0796 — **kb-msg Tier-2 relay was mirrored into the tap but never installed onto consumer boxes.** `msg-client.sh` and `msg-client.js` shipped correctly into `share/scripts/` (XACA-0777), but `install_helper_scripts()` in `libexec/installers/install-shell.sh` copies to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/scripts/` from a hard-coded **allowlist**, and neither name was on it. Mirroring a file into `share/` is necessary but *not sufficient* — anything a consumer must execute has to be named in that allowlist too. The failure is silent by construction: `fleet-reporter.sh` resolves its relay helper as a **sibling** (`local client="$reporter_dir/msg-client.sh"`), so on every consumer install the sibling simply was not there and the Tier-2 path fell through its "not shipped to this box yet" guard — no error, no warning, just a permanently dark relay. **Fix:** added `msg-client.sh msg-client.js` to the allowlist, with a comment recording the sibling-resolution contract so the coupling is visible to the next editor. Verified by running the patched `install_helper_scripts()` against a sandboxed `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` (all three of `fleet-reporter.sh`/`msg-client.sh`/`msg-client.js` land executable), plus a negative control against the unpatched function that reproduces both absences — confirming the check is not vacuously green.
- XACA-0796 (follow-up, PR #698 review) — **Shipping the two entrypoints was necessary but still not sufficient: the sibling contract has a SECOND hop.** `msg-client.js:42` carries a *top-level, non-lazy* `require('./vault-keygen.js')`, and `msg-client.sh:41` bootstraps `libsodium-wrappers` with a bare `npm install` in `$SCRIPT_DIR`. Neither `vault-keygen.js` nor the client `package.json` was in `share/scripts/` or the allowlist. The result was a **regression in failure mode**, not merely an incomplete fix: with `msg-client.sh` now present, `fleet-reporter.sh`'s `[ -x "$client" ]` guard *passes*, execution proceeds, and node dies on `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` inside a swallowed `2>/dev/null || true` (`fleet-reporter.sh:753`) — a silent crash every reporter cycle, replacing the honest short-circuit the missing file used to give. Guard 2 (vault-key check) spares un-provisioned boxes, so the damage landed precisely on the machines actually provisioned for kb-msg. Meanwhile `npm install` in the flattened consumer layout found no `package.json`, walked up, and installed nothing — the futile npm+node startup Guard 2 exists to prevent. **Fix:** `sync_file` `vault-keygen.js` + client `package.json` into `share/scripts/` (canonical `sync-tap.sh` first, per XACA-0340), and add them to `install_helper_scripts()` — `vault-keygen.js` to the executable loop, `package.json` to a new **data-file loop that copies without `chmod +x`** (a package manifest is not an executable). Verified at the **execution layer**, which is the layer the original presence-only assertions structurally could not reach: the installed `msg-client.js` now resolves its full require chain (terminates at `Error: fetch failed`, i.e. every module loaded and it reached the network), `npm install` in the installed dir resolves the manifest and lands `libsodium-wrappers`, and the wrapper end-to-end reports no module errors. Prior negative control still reproduces `Cannot find module './vault-keygen.js'` against the pre-fix tree.
- XACA-0796 (PR #698 fourth-pass review) — **BLOCKER: the fail-open guard was fail-CLOSED, and lying about it.** The unreadable-mtime branch set `_stamp=0` **and** `_now=0`, making `elapsed = 0` — which is not "expired", it is "created this instant", i.e. maximally IN cooldown. Since the enclosing block already gates on `COOLDOWN -gt 0`, `0 -lt COOLDOWN` is unconditionally true, so that path **always** hit `exit 1` while printing "ignoring cooldown". The comment three lines above promised "fail OPEN — never wedge the relay shut on a stat quirk"; the code did the exact opposite, reachable via the `[ -f ]`/`stat` TOCTOU window that a 60s LaunchAgent probes 1440x/day. Fixed by dropping `_now=0` (it already holds `$(date +%s)`, so elapsed becomes the full epoch = expired). **Root cause of why it survived four review rounds: the backoff had ZERO test coverage.** Now covered by T6 in `tests/test-msg-client-install.sh` (suite 16/16): T6a fresh sentinel short-circuits with npm never invoked; T6b expired sentinel releases; T6c `COOLDOWN=0` overrides; T6d non-numeric warns and still enforces; **T6e reaches the fail-open branch itself** via a controlled mutant that neutralises the mtime probe (the same technique as T5), asserting the script PROCEEDS rather than exiting "in cooldown". T6e is verified non-vacuous: reintroducing `_now=0` makes it fail with the exact diagnosis, and removing it makes it pass. **Two of these tests were themselves vacuous when first written** — T6b and T6c set `MSG_CLIENT_NO_AUTO_INSTALL=1`, which exits ABOVE the cooldown block, so they passed without ever reaching the logic under test; they now assert on the "Installing client dependencies" marker printed immediately before npm runs, and fail loudly if the gate is never reached. That is the third distinct sighting of vacuous-green in this one ticket, in three different forms: an uncounted assertion, an exit code gated only on failures, and a test whose fixture short-circuits past its own subject.

- XACA-0796 (PR #698, uncounted-assertion fix) — **`test-validate-install.sh` had a test that ran and was never counted.** `_val_check_config: fails for malformed JSON (with jq)` called `test_start` and `assert_equal` but never `test_pass` on the jq branch. Because the harness's `assert_*` helpers only record on FAILURE, on any machine WITH jq the test executed, passed, and was scored as nothing — surfacing as `Total: 82 / Passed: 81 / Failed: 0`. The `Failed: 0` masked it, and the no-jq `else` branch *did* call `test_pass`, so the one-off gap read like a rounding quirk instead of an uncounted assertion. Pre-existing (byte-identical at baseline `f2b34a9`) and not introduced by this ticket, but fixed here because this ticket already modifies the file and because it is the same vacuous-green class as the `PASSED`-floor gate added earlier: an assertion that cannot be counted is indistinguishable from an assertion that was never written. Suite now reports 82/82/0.

- XACA-0796 (PR #698 third-pass review) — **Hardened the npm backoff: two silent fail-open paths and a `stat` portability trap.** (1) **`stat` flag order was actively dangerous, not merely non-firing.** The chain tried BSD `stat -f %m` before GNU `stat -c %Y`. On GNU coreutils `-f` is `--file-system` and `%m` is the **mount point**, so on Linux it would have *succeeded* and returned a path string — which then feeds `$(( _now - _stamp ))` and breaks the arithmetic, rather than failing over to the correct form. Reversed to `-c` first: BSD rejects `-c` outright ("illegal option") and falls through cleanly, so the GNU-first order is safe on both. Latent only (all-macOS fleet today), but it would have surfaced as a broken backoff on the first Linux runner. (2) **Non-numeric `MSG_CLIENT_BOOTSTRAP_COOLDOWN` silently disabled the backoff** — it now warns and falls back to 3600s rather than leaving a 60s retry storm running with no explanation. (3) **An unwritable install dir silently defeated the sentinel** (`|| true` swallowed it), returning the box to per-cycle retries with no signal; it now warns loudly that backoff is DISABLED and names the remedy, while staying non-fatal so an unwritable dir never wedges the relay for someone who fixes npm later. The sentinel write is wrapped in a subshell so the SHELL's own redirect-failure message is suppressed too — `: > f 2>/dev/null` still leaks "Permission denied" because that error is emitted during redirect setup, not by the command. (4) An unreadable mtime now fails **open** explicitly (cooldown treated as expired) with a warning, rather than relying on an undefined arithmetic result. Every failure mode in this bootstrap now fails open — the relay must never wedge shut on a `stat` quirk — but none of them fails **silently**. Verified in five states: cooldown short-circuits with npm never invoked, `COOLDOWN=0` overrides, backdated sentinel expires, non-numeric override warns and still enforces backoff, unwritable dir warns with no raw shell error leaking.

- XACA-0796 (PR #698 re-review follow-ups) — **npm bootstrap guards, dependency pinning, and doctor coverage.** (1) `msg-client.sh`'s libsodium bootstrap ran with no `command -v npm` guard and no failure backoff. It executes under `fleet-reporter.sh`, which a LaunchAgent fires **every 60s** and whose caller swallows both stderr and exit code (`2>/dev/null || true`) — so a failing bootstrap was invisible *and* repeated ~1440x/day. Added a fail-fast npm check (exit 127) plus a cooldown sentinel (`.msg-client-bootstrap-failed`, default 3600s, `MSG_CLIENT_BOOTSTRAP_COOLDOWN` overrides, sentinel removed on success). Verified: a fresh sentinel short-circuits with npm never invoked, `COOLDOWN=0` overrides, and a backdated sentinel is ignored past expiry. (2) `package-lock.json` now ships alongside `package.json`, so a consumer's `npm install` resolves the exact libsodium-wrappers build we tested rather than every box independently re-resolving `^0.7.16`. (3) `validate-install.sh` gained the relay payload, split into an executable list (`fleet-reporter.sh`, `msg-client.sh` — Guard 1 tests `[ -x ]`, so those bits are load-bearing) and a **presence-only** list (`msg-client.js`, `vault-keygen.js`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json` — these ship 644 by design; demanding `+x` would warn on a *correct* install and train people to ignore doctor). This closes the detection gap: `aiteamforge doctor` previously could not see a recurrence of the allowlist-gap class, because the entrypoint is present and it is the transitive sibling that goes missing. Two new assertions in `test-validate-install.sh` simulate exactly that defect; one existing fixture was rewritten to write the full payload and then remove a single file, keeping it a one-variable probe instead of silently re-testing whatever the payload grows into. Suite 82 tests / 0 failures (was 80/0). Note: `test-doctor-fix.sh` + `test-installers.sh` show 10 pre-existing failures on a clean tree at `279ace4` — unchanged by this work, confirmed by baseline, and out of scope here.

- XACA-0796 (PR #698 review follow-ups) — **New execution-level regression test `tests/test-msg-client-install.sh` (11/11), plus honest mode bits.** (1) The test runs `install_helper_scripts()` against a sandboxed `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` and then **executes** `msg-client.js` from the installed layout, asserting the require chain resolves — the layer presence-only assertions structurally cannot reach. T5 is a negative control that strips *only* `vault-keygen.js` and asserts `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` returns; it caught a bug in its own first draft (stripping `msg-client.js` too made node fail on the *entrypoint*, so T5 passed for the wrong reason), which is precisely why it exists. Registered in `tests/ci-manifest` as `plain-shell`; the manifest-completeness gate passes. Assertions are counted and the `Passed:` total is printed, so a 0/0 run cannot masquerade as green. (2) `chmod +x` is no longer applied to `msg-client.js` / `vault-keygen.js` / `package.json` — none has a shebang and none is ever exec'd directly (`msg-client.js` is reached via `exec node "$CLIENT_JS"`, `vault-keygen.js` via `require()`), so the bit advertised a contract that does not exist. `msg-client.sh` stays executable because `fleet-reporter.sh`'s Guard 1 tests `[ -x "$client" ]` — that one bit is load-bearing.
- XACA-0810 — **`kb-knowledge-search`'s batched-matching rewrite (XACA-0721) had never reached `kanban-helpers.template.sh`, so consumer boxes were still running the pre-0721 per-file walk.** The function exists in THREE independent copies — canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` plus two tap mirrors (`share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`, `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`) — and **neither tap copy is in `sync-tap.sh`'s mirror map**, so no drift gate has ever compared them against canonical. A three-way behavioural diff of the whole function found XACA-0754/0760 (local-root merges) and XACA-0800 (zero-result hint, OR-fallback, `--` end-of-options terminator) genuinely in parity across all three — the XACA-0800 parity claim was independently verified rather than assumed — but XACA-0721 present in canonical and the aliases copy and **absent from the template copy**, which still ran the original walk: a tag `grep -m1`, a filename `grep -Fqi`, and a body `grep -Fqi` fired **per .md file**, versus the batched form's fixed number of passes per root (one recursive `grep -rH '^tags:'`, one recursive `grep -rliF` carrying one `-e` per term for the multi-term OR-union, and a pure-shell basename scan) followed by a single `awk` title/tags extract over only the matched set. Ported verbatim from canonical, including the pre-loop scratch declarations — which must stay OUT of the loop because a zsh `local` inside a loop leaks the variable to stdout (knowledge k501) and would corrupt `--porcelain` TSV and `--json` output. Result SET and `result_count` are unchanged by design; only intra-root ordering changes (`sort -u` lexical, vs. `find`'s directory-entry order), and cross-root tier precedence (project > team > subject > agent) is untouched.
- XACA-0810 — **Recorded the one deliberate tap-vs-canonical divergence in-file, so the next audit does not "fix" it.** The telemetry log dir is `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR}/kanban-logs` in both tap copies but `${AITEAMFORGE_DIR:-$HOME/dev-team}/kanban-logs` in canonical. The `$HOME/dev-team` fallback is a DEV-MACHINE default: on a consumer box there is no `~/dev-team` checkout, so letting it fire would manufacture the phantom `~/dev-team/kanban-logs` tree that XACA-0746 removed here and that XACA-0760's own canonical-side comment cites as its rationale. `AITEAMFORGE_DIR` is always set by the tap's shell init before either file is sourced, so the fallback is unreachable-by-design on consumers and only harmful if it ever did fire. Left divergent on purpose, now with an explicit `INTENTIONAL DIVERGENCE FROM CANONICAL` comment in both copies citing XACA-0746/0760/0340. The aliases copy's separate `_kb_get_team`-instead-of-`_kb_detect_context` persona resolution was already documented in-file as a deliberate context-safe adaptation and is likewise unchanged.
- XACA-0788 — Ports the XACA-0778 crash-recovery reconciliation engine into the tap-shipped `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` so consumers who source that helper get working `kb-recover` / `kb-resume <ID>` commands. XACA-0778 added the engine to canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` (which is dev-only / not `sync-tap.sh`-mapped, XACA-0632) and mirrored only the LCARS "NEEDS RECONNECT" lane + clean-shutdown sentinel into `share/`, leaving the reconciliation engine itself unshipped — the tap was shipping the UI surface of a feature with no engine behind it. Ports 7 functions byte-faithfully from canonical: `_kb_tmux_live_window_ids`, `_kb_reconcile_plan_doc_path`, `_kb_reconcile_inprogress` (the pure read-only BOUND/ORPHANED classifier), `kb-reconcile-inprogress` (CLI wrapper), `kb-recover` (orphaned-work resume manifest, `--team`/`--all-teams`/`--json`), `_kb_resume_rebind`, and an arg-dispatch graft onto the existing `kb-resume` so `kb-resume <ID>` re-binds an orphan to the current window while the no-arg pause/resume toggle is unchanged. No `~/dev-team` coupling introduced (all path resolution goes through the template's existing `_kb_get_board_file` / `_kb_get_team_from_code` / `_kb_detect_context` helpers; the only `~/` refs are `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` in `--help` text). New sandboxed `tests/test-xaca-0788-crash-recovery-in-tap.sh` (16 assertions, `plain-shell`): renders + sources the template under a `TEST_TMP_DIR` sandbox, asserts all 7 functions defined + the kb-resume graft, exercises the classifier's BOUND-vs-ORPHANED verdict against a `KB_BOARD_FILE` scratch board + ephemeral tmux session (per project knowledge P088), proves the read-only guarantee (`cmp`/`cksum` board-unchanged), the missing-board `[]` contract, and graceful no-tmux degradation — with explicit pass-counter + negative controls to defeat the vacuous-green harness trap. **Scope note:** this ports the engine into the ONE shipped helper that structurally supports it (the template carries the full `kb-run`/`activeWindows` work-tracking subsystem). The preferred fresh-install runtime file `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh` lacks that subsystem entirely, and the already-mirrored `server.py` LCARS lane resolves helpers via a hardcoded `~/dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh` that never exists on a consumer — so the lane stays dark on consumers regardless of this port. Those two consumer-activation gaps are tracked as follow-up XACA-0816; the SessionStart crash-detect nudge hook is XACA-0817.
- XACA-0803 — **port-drift guard named the wrong side authoritative.** `share/scripts/lcars-launch-helpers.sh`'s Check 2 in `_lcars_port_drift_guard` compared `team-paths.json`'s allocated port against the deprecated `DEFAULT_TEAMS` baked-in value and called every legitimately band-allocated port (XACA-0463's next-free-in-band allocator) "drift", recommending `kb-port-reconcile --prefer canonical <team>` — advice that would have moved a healthy team off the port its running server, `.port` file, and tmux session all agreed on. Check 2 is now band-aware: silent when the resolved port falls inside the team's half-open allocated band `[base, base+range)`, and warns only on genuinely unresolvable states (no band declared for the team, or a missing/non-numeric resolved port), with remediation corrected to the read-only `kb-port-reconcile --check --team <team>`. Mirrors the canonical fix (`scripts/lcars-launch-helpers.sh`), verified by `scripts/tests/test-xaca-0803-port-drift-guard-authority.sh` (79/79). Also mirrors the accompanying authority-terminology pass in `share/scripts/kb-port-reconcile` (header docs: `team-paths.json` labelled the authoritative registry, `DEFAULT_TEAMS` labelled a deprecated fallback, and `--prefer canonical` labelled a narrow escape hatch that must not be suggested without proof of registry corruption) and `share/scripts/lcars-health-check.sh` (comments disambiguated — that script used the bare word "canonical" for the *resolved/registry* port, the opposite of the guard's former usage; behaviour unchanged, one non-asserted warning string reworded).

## [0.17.8] - 2026-07-14

### Fixed
- XACA-0734 — **The applicability gate now reads the KEY, not the file — and fails OPEN on every degraded input (PR #693 review, XACA-0734-010 / XACA-0734-012).** `_xaca0734_launchagents_applicable()` detected a declined-kanban install with `grep -qE '"lcars_kanban"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*false'` — a flat, file-WIDE substring match with no notion of a JSON key. Correct against today's schema; silently catastrophic against tomorrow's. ANY second occurrence of that byte pattern anywhere in `.aiteamforge-config` — an audit-history array recording a PAST `false`, a per-team override, a stale key — would close the gate on a box whose LIVE key is `true`. And it closes it in **the one direction this gate must never fail in**: a false-OPEN installs an agent the user did not want (visible, one line in the sentinel to undo), while a false-CLOSE suppresses every mandatory agent — *including `com.aiteamforge.auto-upgrade.plist`, the agent that performs upgrades* — while every check still reports the box healthy. That turns this entire ticket into a silent no-op on exactly the machines it exists to fix. Note setup also writes the bare string `lcars_kanban` as an element of the `installed_features` ARRAY in the same file, so a substring match was always one edit away from confusing an array element with a key. **Fix:** a key-scoped `jq` read of `.features.lcars_kanban`, using the house tool for this file (`bin/aiteamforge-doctor.sh`, `bin/aiteamforge-setup.sh`'s upgrade-hydration both parse it with `jq`; `doctor` already treats a missing `jq` as a hard FAIL). The defining property is **FAIL OPEN ON EVERY DEGRADED INPUT**: missing file, unreadable file, empty file, truncated/malformed JSON, a non-object root, a missing `features` block, a missing key, or **no `jq` on PATH at all** — every one resolves to APPLICABLE. Only an explicit, correctly-parsed `features.lcars_kanban === false` closes the gate. There is deliberately **no `grep` fallback** when `jq` is absent: reinstating the flat grep on already-degraded boxes would reinstate the false-CLOSE hazard precisely where it is least affordable — a "fallback" that can fail closed is not a fallback, it is the bug with a longer code path.
- XACA-0734 — **The batch-uninstall guard is now EXPORTED, so it survives a process boundary (PR #693 review, XACA-0734-011).** `uninstall_kanban_system` is `export -f`'d, so running the teardown from a separate bash process (`bash -c`, an `xargs`/`find -exec` wrapper, a future installer that shells out) is a legitimate, reachable shape — but `_XACA0734_BATCH_UNINSTALL` was a plain shell variable, which does not cross that boundary. The child would have run the entire batch with the guard UNSET, every `uninstall_<x>_launchagent()` would have fallen through to `_xaca0734_record_optout`, and the sentinel would have come out listing every mandatory agent: the poisoned, self-sealing box that BLOCKING 2 just closed, reintroduced by nothing more than the caller's choice of invocation. The flag is now `export`ed (and still `unset` after the teardown, so a later targeted uninstall in the same shell records normally). Covered by a test that drives the record path across a real `bash -c` boundary, plus a control proving the boundary is genuinely lossy without the export.
- XACA-0734 — **`_xaca0734_launchagents_skip_reason` now names the way back IN (PR #693 review, XACA-0734-013).** It reported WHY mandatory LaunchAgents were suppressed (cockpit profile / kanban declined) but never how to reverse it. Telling a user their agents are suppressed without telling them how to un-suppress them is how they conclude they are stuck. Both branches now name the recovery path — `re-run: aiteamforge setup` — which is a REAL path, not a hopeful suggestion: setup's upgrade-hydration block forces `INSTALL_KANBAN="yes"` on a non-cockpit re-run and rewrites both markers this gate reads. This is the mirror of `_xaca0734_print_optout_hint`, which prints the opt-OUT command at the moment an agent is about to be materialized; the opt-IN path now prints at the moment we report agents being withheld.
- XACA-0734 — **Refreshed two stale assertions in `tests/test-doctor-fix.sh` (PR #693 review, XACA-0734-014).** The suite asserted that `doctor --fix` prints `"Auto-fix not yet implemented"` and references `"setup"` in its remediation guidance. Neither has held since **XACA-0655** (`33a73ff`) made `--fix` actually remediate — the stub string is absent from the doctor entirely, and `"setup"` now appears only when a board/setup-KIND check happens to fail, which varies by machine. (Both fail identically on the v0.17.7 baseline `ed53095`, so they are **not** a XACA-0734 regression; XACA-0734 is merely the ticket whose test run noticed.) They are stale, not broken, and asserting the stub's presence now asserts a *regression*. Refreshed to the durable post-0655 contract: with failures present, `--fix` must always report its outcome (a `Remediation Summary (--fix)` section, or an explicit "No auto-remediable issues detected"), and must tag its actions (`[fix]` auto-applied / `[manual]` run-it-yourself) — while the removed stub string must never come back. Also contains the suite's launchd blast radius (**XACA-0787**): now that `--fix` really does render + `launchctl load` a keepalive plist, the suite exports `AITEAMFORGE_SKIP_LAUNCHCTL=1` so a sandbox plist can never register a live job in the developer's real launchd session.
- XACA-0734 — **Sentinel lifecycle, and the primary way to populate it: hand-editing.** The sentinel (`~/.aiteamforge/launchagents.optout`) is a supported, user-editable file — `echo "<agent>.plist" >> ~/.aiteamforge/launchagents.optout` is the documented way to decline a mandatory agent. `update_launchagents()` and `doctor`'s `keepalive` fix both print this exact command, with the real resolved sentinel path, whenever they are about to materialize a missing mandatory agent — deliberately no new CLI subcommand was added for this. Each `install_<x>_launchagent()` (`libexec/installers/install-kanban.sh`) now CHECKS the sentinel at entry and refuses to install when opted out; `uninstall_<x>_launchagent()` RECORDS an opt-out (deduped). **Follow-up correction (same-review catch):** an earlier version of this fix had `install_<x>_launchagent()` unconditionally CLEAR the opt-out on every call, on the theory that "installing something is an explicit opt back in". That was wrong: `install_kanban_system()` (the installers' only caller) runs on every `aiteamforge setup`, including the explicit `reconfigure` path, not just a fresh install — and calls every `install_<x>_launchagent()` unconditionally regardless of what the user actually asked for. Auto-clearing there meant a hand-recorded opt-out would be silently reversed the next time the user reconfigured for something unrelated (e.g. adding a team). Opting back in is now only ever a deliberate sentinel edit. **Critical teardown (REVISED — see the batch-uninstall entry below):** the batch teardown `uninstall_kanban_system()` no longer RECORDS opt-outs at all, so it no longer needs (and no longer performs) a compensating sentinel wipe. The full-uninstall path `remove_launchagents()` in `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-uninstall.sh` still DELETES the sentinel wholesale, which is correct there: the entire installation is going away, and a populated sentinel that outlived it would suppress every mandatory agent on the next fresh install. Note the sentinel lives under `~/.aiteamforge/` (the runtime config dir), NOT under `WORKING_DIR` (`~/aiteamforge`), so `remove_files()` cannot clean it up implicitly.
- XACA-0734 — **Applicability gate: installs that RECORDED "no LaunchAgents" are now honored (PR #693 review, BLOCKING).** The first cut of this fix read the opt-out sentinel and nothing else, which reproduced *this ticket's own bug with the roles reversed*: intent WAS recorded, the new code just did not read the file it was recorded in. Two supported install shapes deliberately have ZERO LaunchAgents, and both record that at setup time: (a) **cockpit** installs (`bin/aiteamforge-setup.sh` skips feature selection, never calls `install_kanban_system`, explicitly skips its LaunchAgent load block, and writes `${WORKING_DIR}/.install-profile` = `cockpit` precisely so downstream tools can skip deliberately-absent components — `bin/aiteamforge-doctor.sh` already reported the agent-free state as a PASS); and (b) installs where the user answered **no** to *"Install LCARS Kanban?"*, which is recorded as `"lcars_kanban": false` in `${WORKING_DIR}/.aiteamforge-config`. Without a gate, every `aiteamforge upgrade` on such a box would have rendered and `launchctl load`ed all three mandatory agents — two of which reference `${WORKING_DIR}/lcars-ui` and the kanban backup script, which do not exist there — turning a healthy machine into a source of recurring FAILING launchd jobs, while `aiteamforge doctor` simultaneously went red with three FAILs on a state the other doctor called a PASS. New `_xaca0734_launchagents_applicable()` (`libexec/lib/launchagents.sh`) reads those two markers and gates the row-2 materialize, the assert-present pass (`upgrade`), the mandatory-presence loop (`doctor`), and `--fix`'s `keepalive` render. It **fails OPEN** — a box with neither marker (i.e. every pre-existing install, including the M1Pro box that motivated this ticket) is applicable, because inferring intent from a marker's absence is the original bug. Row 1 (refreshing a plist that already exists) stays UNCONDITIONAL, and `--force` does not punch through the gate. Deliberately marker-based rather than writing the declined agents into the opt-out sentinel: the sentinel's primary population path is the user's own text editor, and any "clear it on a later yes" logic could silently stomp a hand-edited opt-out. The markers are read-only here and setup rewrites them on every run, so a decliner who later re-runs `aiteamforge setup` (which forces `INSTALL_KANBAN=yes` on upgrade-mode re-runs) gets the agents back with no sentinel involvement at all.
- XACA-0734 — **An aborted uninstall can no longer permanently seal a box (PR #693 review, BLOCKING).** `libexec/installers/install-kanban.sh` runs under `set -euo pipefail`. Each `uninstall_<x>_launchagent()` APPENDED its agent to the opt-out sentinel, and only the END of the batch `uninstall_kanban_system()` wiped the sentinel again to undo it. Any non-zero exit — or a **Ctrl-C**, an entirely normal response to an uninstall started by mistake — landing between the first record and the final wipe left the sentinel listing EVERY mandatory agent. By this ticket's own (correct) design nothing downstream ever clears that: installers refuse to install an opted-out agent and `upgrade` skips it. So `uninstall` → `^C` → `reinstall` produced a box on which `com.aiteamforge.auto-upgrade.plist` could never be installed by ANY code path — the original self-sealing failure, relocated to the uninstall→reinstall path. **Fix:** the batch path no longer records at all. `uninstall_kanban_system()` sets `_XACA0734_BATCH_UNINSTALL=1` for the duration of the teardown and the helpers route through the new `_xaca0734_record_optout_unless_batch()`, which is a no-op while it is set. "Remove the kanban system" says nothing about which agents you want on a future reinstall, so recording one per agent was inventing intent nobody expressed. With nothing written there is nothing to undo, so an abort leaves nothing behind — a strictly stronger guarantee than a compensating `trap`. The batch's trailing `_xaca0734_clear_all_optouts()` is removed with it: now that the batch records nothing, its only remaining effect would have been to silently destroy the user's HAND-EDITED opt-outs. A TARGETED single-agent uninstall still records normally.
- XACA-0734 — **The opt-out sentinel is hand-edited, so it is now parsed like a human wrote it (XACA-0734-002).** `_xaca0734_is_opted_out()` compared each line with a raw `[ "$line" = "$agent" ]`, so `agent.plist\r` (CRLF endings), `  agent.plist  ` (stray spaces), or a tab-indented entry all silently FAILED to match — re-materializing an agent the user had explicitly declined. Since hand-editing is the PRIMARY (currently the only) way the sentinel is populated, that was the same silent-intent-loss class the ticket exists to kill. Lines are now normalized before comparison (trailing `\r` stripped, leading/trailing whitespace trimmed), blank lines are skipped, and whole-line `#` comments are ignored — and honored as comments, so a commented-out entry no longer suppresses its agent. `_xaca0734_clear_optout()` uses the same normalized matching (an entry that `is_opted_out` can SEE must be removable, or a user could be stuck opted out with no way back) and PRESERVES the user's comments and blank lines when rewriting.
- XACA-0734 — **`_xaca0734_clear_optout()` no longer truncates an unreadable sentinel (PR #693 review).** It ran `grep -v -x -F "$agent" "$f" > "$tmp" 2>/dev/null || true` and then `mv`'d UNCONDITIONALLY. `grep` exits 1 for "no lines selected" (normal — the sentinel held only that one agent) but 2 for a genuine error such as an unreadable file; the `|| true` swallowed both identically, so a read failure produced an EMPTY tempfile that was moved over the original, silently destroying every recorded opt-out. The rewrite reads the file explicitly, bails with a warning (non-zero) when it is unreadable or the tempfile cannot be written, and never replaces the sentinel on a failed rewrite. Relatedly (XACA-0734-003), `_xaca0734_record_optout()`'s `printf … >> "$f"` was unguarded while the `mkdir -p` above it was `|| true`'d — under `set -euo pipefail` a failed write would abort the whole script rather than report; both the `mkdir` and the append are now checked and warn on failure.
- XACA-0734 — **Load-verify is now an exact match, and the success path is actually tested (PR #693 review).** `launchctl load` returns 0 even when launchd REJECTS the job, so registration is confirmed via `launchctl list`. That check was `grep -q "$label"`, which is wrong twice: `grep -q` takes a REGEX (every label is full of dots, so `com.aiteamforge.lcars-health` matched `comXaiteamforgeYlcars-health`) and it is a SUBSTRING match on the line (so an unrelated `…lcars-health-check` or `…lcars-health.disabled` entry reported the agent as loaded). Both fail toward the same wrong answer — claiming an agent registered when it did not. New shared `_xaca0734_launchctl_is_loaded()` matches the label exactly against `launchctl list`'s final field (labels never contain whitespace, so no regex escaping is needed) and is used by `upgrade`, both doctors, and the render+load helper. The test suite's `launchctl` stub emitted NO output, so `launchctl list | grep -q` could only ever answer "not loaded" and the successful `"Installed and loaded"` branch had never once executed; the stub now emits a realistic `PID/Status/Label` listing driven by a `LAUNCHCTL_LOADED` variable, so both the registered and the rejected paths are genuinely exercised.
- XACA-0734 — **`bin/aiteamforge-doctor.sh` ported to the shared mandatory set (PR #693 review).** It was a FIFTH LaunchAgent sibling and the last one still asking only *"is it loaded?"* — reporting a plist that does not EXIST (nothing will ever load it; the M1Pro state) as a mere "not loaded" warning, indistinguishable from a plist that merely needs a re-login. It now sources `libexec/lib/launchagents.sh` (defensively — a partially-upgraded install falls back to the legacy checks rather than crashing) and reports MISSING mandatory agents as failures with an actionable fix, honors the opt-out sentinel (an opted-out agent's absence is a PASS), and shares the same applicability gate, so the two doctors can no longer contradict each other on the same machine. In `libexec/commands/aiteamforge-doctor.sh`, `check_launchagents()` also stopped DOUBLE-REPORTING a missing plist: the mandatory-presence loop already reports it as a FAIL with real remediation, so the per-agent "not loaded" warning now fires only when the plist is actually present — one defect, one line, instead of a FAIL plus a WARN that read like two unrelated problems.
- **XACA-0777 / XACA-0785 — regenerate stale tap mirrors from canonical.** XACA-0777 (kb-msg) landed its canonical `fleet-reporter.sh` / fleet-monitor server changes on `develop` and added the `sync_file` mappings for `msg-client.{js,sh}` / `msg-store.py`, but never ran `sync-tap.sh` — so the mirrors were stale and the five new files were never materialized into the tap at all. `sync-tap-drift` consequently failed on `develop` itself and on every branch cut from it. Re-ran `sync-tap.sh` to regenerate all 8 from canonical: 0 drifted, 0 missing. Consumer boxes would otherwise have been missing `msg-client.{js,sh}`, `msg-store.py`, and `msg-relay-routes.js` entirely.
- **XACA-0794 — the board-less `mainevent` alias is now self-describing, and `aiteamforge-paths init` no longer crashes on it.** `mainevent` is a deliberate BOARD-LESS ALIAS (XACA-0727): it owns no kanban board — `command` does — but it does own LCARS port 8400, team_code `MEV`, the `mainevent-<project>` crew launcher, and its persona group. Its absent `kanban_dir` is correct BY DESIGN. But JSON carries no comments, so every reader who opened `team-paths.json`, saw the empty `kanban_dir`, and reasoned from the data alone concluded the entry was corrupt — which happened three separate times (the XACA-0724 gate spec told auditors to grep telemetry for a `persona='mainevent'` string no code path emits; ticket XACA-0794 was itself filed as "remove the phantom team"; and a doc written mid-ticket asserted the launcher binds 8400, which it does not). The entry now carries explicit `"board_less": true` / `"alias_of": "command"` markers in `DEFAULT_TEAMS`, and `_backfill_board_less_markers_on_disk()` (wired into `load_config()`) heals existing `~/.aiteamforge/team-paths.json` overlays on read — strictly additive, idempotent, backup-first, and safe on consumer boxes whose tap-seeded overlay writes the literal `"null"` sentinel. Deliberately NOT added to the tap's own `_aiteamforge_default_teams` seed: that is a positional 7-column tab table (`printf '%s\t%s\t…'`) with no slot for a named field, and adding one would break the `IFS=$'\t' read` parser contract — the read-path migration converges BOTH seeds onto the marker shape instead, which removes the two-sources-in-lockstep drift risk rather than doubling it. `get_team_kanban_dir()` / `get_team_working_dir()` now check the marker FIRST and name the alias target in the `KeyError`, retaining the legacy `None`/`""`/`"null"` inference as a fallback for un-migrated overlays. **Live crash fixed:** `share/scripts/aiteamforge-paths-init.sh` used an unguarded `entry["kanban_dir"]` subscript, so `aiteamforge-paths init` wrote the config and then died with `KeyError: 'kanban_dir'` while listing teams — non-zero exit, truncated output. It now renders the absence in words (`mainevent  (board-less alias — use 'command')  (LCARS :8400)`). 29 regression tests lock the contract — including that `board_less: true` WINS over any `kanban_dir` value re-added beside it — so a fourth "cleanup" attempt fails loudly instead of silently resurrecting the phantom `mainevent-board.json` bug XACA-0727 fixed.
- **XACA-0794 — the shell resolver on CONSUMER boxes never had a board-less guard, and silently answered with Academy's board (XACA-0794-013).** The two tap-side copies of `_kb_get_kanban_dir` (`share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`, `share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`) are INDEPENDENT resolvers from the dev tree's `kanban-helpers.sh` — and XACA-0727's board-less guard only ever landed on the dev copy. So on an installed box a `kb-*` call for `mainevent` read `kanban_dir: null` from the registry, failed Strategy 1's `[[ -d ]]` existence test, matched no case arm (there is no `mainevent` arm), and fell through to the Strategy-3 catch-all — silently resolving to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/kanban` and operating on **Academy's board**. Not a clean failure: a wrong answer against a bystander team's data. Both copies now carry a Strategy-0 board-less guard that refuses BEFORE any resolution strategy runs and names the alias target (`kb: 'mainevent' is a board-less alias … Use 'command' instead.`, rc=1). The guard's `_kb_board_less_alias_of` mirrors Python's `board_less_alias_of()` exactly — explicit overlay marker first, built-in `DEFAULT_TEAMS` table second — so an overlay that marks a team `board_less: true` but omits `alias_of` still resolves the target instead of dropping the most useful half of the error message. Applied mechanically to both copies from one source string, because this function's own header names it a known three-copy sibling-drift site. All 14 pre-existing cases in `tests/test-xaca-0649-kanban-dir-resolver.sh` still pass — no existing team's resolution changes.
- **XACA-0794 — the three self-healing config-rewrite passes shared three bugs, so they now share one write path (XACA-0794-008/-009/-012).** `_backfill_a1_fields_on_disk` (XACA-0522), `_scrub_contract_violating_keys_on_disk` (XACA-0643), and `_backfill_board_less_markers_on_disk` (XACA-0794) each rewrite `team-paths.json` during `load_config()`, and each was written by cloning the previous one — so each carried its own copy of the same lock/TOCTOU/backup/atomic-write skeleton and therefore its own copy of the same three defects (sibling-heuristic drift, knowledge k501; patching three copies would only have seeded a fourth). The skeleton now lives once in `_rewrite_config_on_disk` / `_atomic_write_json`; the passes supply only a predicate, a transform, a backup tag, and a log label. **(008) File mode** was reset to the process umask on every self-heal: the tmp file was created fresh and `os.replace()` carried its mode onto the config, so a `0600` `team-paths.json` — which holds per-team Anthropic account ids and API-key env-var names — silently widened to `0644`. The original's mode is now copied onto the tmp file *before* the replace, so content and mode land atomically together. **(009) A symlinked config was destroyed**: `os.replace(tmp, config_path)` replaced the *symlink itself* with a regular file and orphaned the real target, so a config deliberately symlinked into (say) a dotfiles repo silently stopped tracking. The write now resolves the path first and replaces the real file, leaving the symlink intact. **(012) The lock file was unlinked while the flock was still held**, so a racing process could create a fresh lock inode on the same path and both processes would believe they held the lock. Harmless in practice today — the transforms are additive, re-read under the lock, and idempotent, so the worst case was a redundant write — but not a property worth leaving load-bearing. The lock is now taken on the *resolved* path (so a process arriving via a symlink and one arriving via the real file contend for the same inode) and is never unlinked. 12 new regression tests; 8 of them fail against the pre-fix code.
- XACA-0800-011 — **`kb-knowledge-search`'s OR-fallback broke on a leading-dash word-split term (PR #696 review).** The XACA-0800 zero-result OR-fallback word-splits a quoted multi-word phrase into `_retry_argv` and re-invokes the function; if a split token began with `-` (e.g. the realistic natural-language phrase `"how to use -exact matching"` splits into a token `-exact`), the recursive call's own flag parser intercepted it via the `-*)` unknown-flag branch and printed `Unknown flag: …` plus a full `Usage:` dump instead of performing the OR search — and the printed copy-paste "corrected command" reproduced the identical failure when pasted. Not a security/injection issue (the token never reaches `grep`), but it defeated the fallback for exactly the natural-language input class XACA-0800 exists to rescue. Fixed in all three implementations (canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` + both tap-side copies) by assembling the retry argv as recognized flags, followed by a `--` end-of-options terminator, followed by the word-split terms verbatim — and teaching the arg parser to honor a literal `--`: once seen, every remaining token (including one that is itself `--`) is treated as a positional search term, never re-parsed as a flag. The parser change is general — it protects any future caller passing a dash-leading term, not just the recursive retry. Default matching semantics, `--porcelain`/`--json` output, and the once-per-invocation telemetry placement are all unchanged.

### Added
- XACA-0800 — `kb-knowledge-search` in both tap-side copies (`share/templates/aliases/kanban-aliases.sh`, `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh`) gains a zero-result literal-phrase hint + guarded OR-fallback, ported hand-for-hand from the canonical `kanban-helpers.sh` fix (`sync-tap.sh` does not map this function to either tap file — each copy carries its own independent `grep -F` implementation). A quoted multi-word query (e.g. `kb-knowledge-search "Decimal never float quantize"`) is matched as a literal substring, not OR'd words, and almost always returns zero results on a populated corpus (XACA-0793). When `result_count == 0` in human-mode output AND at least one search term contains whitespace, the zero-result footer now prints a one-line explanation plus the corrected OR-form command, then auto-retries the search with the phrase word-split (labelled "no literal match — showing term matches") via a `_KB_KS_NO_FALLBACK`-guarded recursive call with `KB_SEARCH_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` so the retry never double-logs. A single-word zero-result query never triggers the hint (no crying wolf), and `--porcelain`/`--json` output modes are byte-identical to before (no hint, no fallback). The `_retry_argv` array is built as a parallel structure DURING arg-parsing (flags verbatim, positional terms `${=1}`-word-split) rather than reconstructed afterward from `filter_*` vars, avoiding a second divergent source of truth (sibling-heuristic drift, knowledge k501). Telemetry (`kb-search.jsonl`) gains one additive `zero_result_hint:true|false` field; `result_count` on the primary/literal query stays a truthful 0 regardless of whether the fallback found anything. `Usage:`/`--help` text in both files now states the literal-phrase rule up front. Default `grep -F` matching semantics are unchanged in both files — the fix lives entirely in the previously-dead zero-result human-output branch.
- **XACA-0801 — Auto-Planning Gate: tap-side mirror of `_kb_build_planning_gate_section` + wiring.** Ported the canonical auto-planning-gate helper from `~/dev-team/kanban-helpers.sh` into `share/templates/kanban/kanban-helpers.template.sh` — the tap-canonical template that consumer machines render to `$AITEAMFORGE_DIR/kanban-helpers.sh`.
